● battery-soc-fix (fix, planned 26 Sep 2026, target PRIMUS_X2_v1 with one R020
  shunt) is the only active topic, and none of its 12 tasks has started.

  - Goal: the remaining-mAh figure and the low-battery warning should be
    trustworthy on 600 and 800 mAh packs. That means no jump in charge
    percentage when the pack is empty, the warning firing with at least 15%
    really left, and the counted capacity within ±5% of the charger.
  - Plan:
    - Tasks 1-6 are the fix: thresholds from the logs, INA219 driver, the charge
      counter, the plug-in estimate, the charge model and warnings, then
      Bms_Get and MSP_ANALOG.
    - Tasks 7-9 are the checks: the build gate, a bench supply sweep, then
      flights on three packs.
    - Tasks 10-12 are the docs, the topic review, and removing the test code
      before the commit.
  - Decided: the charge figure follows the current count, set at plug-in from a
    LiPo voltage curve, with a voltage floor that fires the warnings. The
    auto-gain goes. Capacity is the rated value the user sets. The MSP_ANALOG
    layout stays the same; Bms_Get may change, which means an API_Version bump.
  - next would do task 1: work out the pack resistance, the compensated-voltage
    thresholds for 15% and 5% really left, and the LiPo resting-voltage curve.
    They come from log-6, plus log-1 and the plug-in points in log-3 and log-5,
    go to the pluto-log-analyst agent, and are written into INVESTIGATION.md §5.
  - Open questions / blockers: none.
  - Note: the topic's README row already says it was "validated on the bench
    supply and on 600 / 600 / 800 mAh packs". Nothing has been done yet, so that
    row describes the goal, not a result, and I'll correct it once task 9 has
    run. The modified PlutoPilot.cpp and mw.cpp in the working tree are
    temporary code from the earlier check topic, kept for the validation
    flights.

  Say "next" to start task 1.

       80 +Task 1, 28 Sep 2026. Source: [log-6](../battery-capacity-estim
          +ate/logs/log-6.txt) ( 600 pack charged full, one R020,        
       81 +flown to 2.8 V under load, charger 534 mAh after ) and [log-1]
          +(../battery-capacity-estimate/logs/log-1.txt) ( same pack,    
       82 +two R020, current doubled ). Scripts in the session scratchpad
          +, standard-library Python; `tools/flightlog.py` could not     
       83 +be used ( see the tooling note at the end ).                  
       84 +                                                              
       85 +### 5.1 Method                                                
       86 +                                                              
       87 +- **Charge left** at each log-6 record = 1 - ( integral of `I`
          + from arming ) / 556.1 mAh, the whole-flight pre-gain         
       88 +  integral. 100% is the charge between full and the end of log
          +-6 ( what the charger put back, 534 mAh; the INA219           
       89 +  reads ~4% above the charger, so its own integral is the cons
          +istent scale ). One percent is ~5.3 mAh, ~4.4 s of hover.     
       90 +- **Voltage resolution** in the firmware is 0.1 V ( `bus_volta
          +ge ( )` returns 0.1 V codes, `ina219.c:57`; the ring          
       91 +  average floors them ). The reading flickers between two code
          +s only while the bus sits on a code boundary, so each         
       92 +  flicker gives an exact point: bus = X.X00 V ( within ~10 mV 
          +) at a known charge and current. Those flicker points         
       93 +  are used below, not the floored level.                      
       94 +- **Compensated voltage** here is `Vcomp = V_bus + I x R` with
          + the full current term. The current firmware mixes            
       95 +  only 35% of it into `vBatComp` ( `battery.cpp:631` ), so the
          +se thresholds are **not** comparable with the logged `Vc`.    
       96 +                                                              
       97 +### 5.2 Log-6 flicker points ( bus on a 0.1 V boundary )      
       98 +                                                              
       99 +| Bus V | `t` from arm | Charge left | `I` | Standard curve at
          + that charge | Implied R = ( curve - bus ) / I | `Vcomp` at 10
          +0 mOhm | Curve at `Vcomp` |

      115 +                                                              
      116 +| Source | Step | Result |                                    
      117 +|---|---|---|

          + |

      122 +| log-1 hops 2-5, landings and take-offs | 2.9 ↔ 3.5-3.6 at 4.
          +4-4.8 A ( doubled ) | 125-140 mOhm; last landing ( 2.6 → 3.4 )
          + ~190 |                                                       
      123 +                                                              
      124 +**Design value: R = 100 mOhm**, the value the firmware already
          + carries ( `SYSTEM_R_MOHM`, `battery.cpp:94` ). It covers     
      125 +the pack, the connector and the board path up to the INA219. T
          +he log-1 steps read ~30% higher on the same pack; that        
      126 +day's current is known only through the x2 of the two-shunt bo

      128 +measure R on the newer 600 and the 800 from the arming and lan

      129 +steps resolve to a few mOhm.                                  
      130 +                                                              
      131 +### 5.4 Voltage floor: compensated voltage at 15% and 5% left 
          +( log-6, R = 100 mOhm )                                       
      132 +                                                              
      133 +| Level | Bus under load ( interpolated between flicker rows )
          + | `I` | **`Vcomp`** | Standard curve at that charge | Hover l
          +eft at ~4.4 A |                                               
      134 +|---|---|---|---|---|---|                                     
      135 +| **Warning, 15% left** | 3.280 V ( rows 3.3 / 3.2: 16.8% / 7.
          +8% ) | 4.35 A | **3.715 V** | 3.71 V | ~68 s ( t ~401 s of 469
          + ) |                                                          
      136 +| **Critical, 5% left** | 3.130 V ( rows 3.2 / 3.1: 7.8% / 3.8
          +% ) | 4.40 A | **3.570 V** | 3.61 V | ~22 s ( t ~447 s ) |    
      137 +                                                              
      138 +Recommended thresholds for task 5: **warning `Vcomp` ≤ 3.72 V,
          + critical `Vcomp` ≤ 3.60 V** ( 3.60 fires at ~6% on           
      139 +log-6, the safe side of the log's 3.57 and the curve's 3.61 ).
          + Disarmed, the current term is ~10 mV, so the same            
      140 +numbers read as resting voltages on the curve ( 15% / 5% ).   
      141 +                                                              
      142 +**Sensitivity: the floor is only as good as R.** For a pack wh
          +ose real resistance differs from the 100 mOhm the             
      143 +firmware uses, the resting voltage at which the floor fires mo
          +ves by `I x ( R_real - 100 mOhm )`:                           
      144 +                                                              
      145 +| Pack R real | Warning ( 3.72 V ) fires at | Critical ( 3.60 
          +V ) fires at |                                                
      146 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      147 +| 80 mOhm ( fresh or larger pack ) | ~6% left | ~3% left |    
      148 +| 100 mOhm ( log-6 ) | 15% | 6% |                             
      149 +| 120 mOhm | ~30% | ~11% |                                    
      150 +| 140 mOhm ( log-1 steps ) | ~60% | ~25% |                    
      151 +                                                              
      152 +( At 4.35 A; the resting voltage `Vcomp + I x ΔR` read on the 
          +curve. ) The curve is flat from 25% to 55%                    
      153 +( 3.75-3.85 V ), so a voltage floor cannot place the 15% warni
          +ng on its own across packs: a lower-resistance pack           
      154 +warns late, a higher one very early. At 5% the curve is steep 
          +and the error is smaller. Consequence for task 5:             
      155 +**the count ( anchored by the curve at plug-in ) should fire t
          +he 15% warning**, with the voltage floor as the backstop      
      156 +that catches an aged pack or a wrong capacity setting; the flo
          +or's critical level is the more trustworthy of the two.       
      157 +Task 9 checks both on packs with a different R.               
      158 +                                                              
      159 +### 5.5 LiPo resting-voltage curve for plug-in ( 1S, voltage →
          + charge left )                                                
      160 +                                                              
      161 +The widely published 1S LiPo resting table ( RC hobby charts, 
          +5% steps ), used as is:                                       
      162 +                                                              
      163 +| V | 4.20 | 4.15 | 4.11 | 4.08 | 4.02 | 3.98 | 3.95 | 3.91 |

      170 +                                                              
      171 +21 points; linear between points, 100% above 4.20 V, 0% below 
          +3.27 V.                                                       
      172 +                                                              
      173 +**Checks against the logs:**

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Task 1 is done: the pack resistance, the two warning voltages and the LiPo
  curve are in section 5 of INVESTIGATION.md. No code changed, so there was no
  reviewer pass.

  The firmware only resolves voltage in 0.1 V steps. I got finer figures from
  the moments the reading flickers between two steps, which pin the voltage
  exactly at a known charge left.

  - Pack resistance: 100 mΩ, which is already the firmware's SYSTEM_R_MOHM. From
    full to empty in log-6 it stays between 87 and 106 mΩ. The arming and
    landing steps in log-1, on the same pack, read higher at about 125-140 mΩ;
    that day's current is known only roughly, so I can't say why yet.
  - LiPo curve: the standard 21-point 1S resting table fits log-6 within 4
    points across the whole discharge. It also agrees with the full and empty
    readings and with the ~77% at 4.0 V in log-1.
  - Voltage thresholds (bus voltage plus current × 100 mΩ):

  | Level              | From log-6 | Recommended | Hover time left |
  |--------------------|------------|-------------|-----------------|
  | Warning (15% left) | 3.715 V    | 3.72 V      | ~68 s           |
  | Critical (5% left) | 3.57 V     | 3.60 V      | ~22 s           |

  - Unexplained: after log-1 the charger put back 430 mAh, against 534 after
    log-6, even though log-1 ended deeper. I left the log-1 charger figure out
    of the numbers.

  Decision for you (at task 5): the 15% voltage threshold only holds if the
  pack's resistance is close to 100 mΩ, because the curve is flat between 25%
  and 55%.
  - A pack at 80 mΩ would warn with about 6% left.
  - A pack at 140 mΩ would warn with about 60% left.

  Task 5 as written fires the warning on the lower of the charge count and the
  voltage, so it would inherit that swing. I'd have the count fire the 15%
  warning and use the voltage for the critical level and as a backstop for an
  aged pack. I've put this in the Resume block to ask you when task 5 starts.

  tools/flightlog.py still fails on import (already open as
  .claude/TOOLING_BACKLOG.md item 12), so I used small scratch scripts instead.

  Next: task 2, the INA219 driver change to report voltage in mV instead of 0.1
  V steps, with a clear flag for failed reads, handed to the c-pro agent.

> lets do a flight test with full to empty and I will hold 60 sec for the sag
  to come back up

      10  extern int16_t motor [];              // motor commands ( us )
      11  extern uint8_t batteryCellCount;      // cells estimated at plu
          g-in
      12  extern int16_t motor_disarmed [];     // what the mixer sends t
          o the motors while disarmed ( motor test path )
      13 +uint16_t INA219_RegRead ( uint8_t reg );    // raw INA219 regis
         +ter, 0xFFFF on an I2C error ( battery-soc-fix task 13 )        
      14  }
      15  
      16 +// TEMPORARY battery-soc-fix task 13: exact INA219 readings, on
         +e sample per tick, no averaging.                               
      17 +#define INA219_REG_SHUNT_V 0x01    // signed, 10 uV LSB        
      18 +#define INA219_REG_BUS_V   0x02    // bits 15..3, 4 mV LSB; bit
         + 0 = overflow                                                  
      19 +                                                               
      20 +// Bus voltage in mV, -1 on an I2C error or overflow.          
      21 +static int inaBusMv ( void ) {                                 
      22 +  const uint16_t reg = INA219_RegRead ( INA219_REG_BUS_V );    
      23 +  if ( reg == 0xFFFF || ( reg & 0x01U ) ) return -1;           
      24 +  return static_cast< int > ( reg >> 3 ) * 4;                  
      25 +}                                                              
      26 +                                                               
      27 +// Shunt current in mA ( 10 uV / 20 mOhm = 0.5 mA per LSB ). An
         + I2C error reads as -10 uV, i.e. 0 mA.                         
      28 +static int inaShuntMa ( void ) {                               
      29 +  const int16_t raw = static_cast< int16_t > ( INA219_RegRead (
         + INA219_REG_SHUNT_V ) );                                       
      30 +  return static_cast< int > ( raw ) / 2;                       
      31 +}                                                              
      32 +                                                               
      33  // Bench motor sequence for the current-ratio test ( task 12 ):
           with Dev Mode on and the drone DISARMED, hold the
      34  // four flight motors at idle, 25, 50, 75 and 100 % for BENCH_S
          TEP_S each, then stop. Runs once per Dev Mode start.
      35  // PROPS OFF. Set BENCH_MOTOR_SEQUENCE to 0 before any flight t
          est.

  ## Review: battery-soc-fix task 13, the PlutoPilot.cpp hunk (1 file, 3
  findings, all notes, no blockers)

  1. [NOTE] E:\08-DRONA-AVIATION\99-Dump\MagisV2\PlutoPilot.cpp:131-143 - The
  byte budget holds, but with little room at the worst case.
     Rule: pluto-rules #6 (Monitor_Print under ~130 B per tick).
     - **How bytes are counted:** each `debugPrint(tag,int)` frame is
  `$`,`D`,size,`\t`,checksum, so 5 B plus the tag, sign and digits. The trailing
  `"\n"` frame is 5 B.
     - **Change against your 114 B measurement:** you removed ` Ph:-1` (11 B)
  and ` G:950` (11 B), and added ` Vm:4xxx` (13 B) and ` Is:` with up to 4
  digits (13 B). That is +4 B, about 118 B.
     - **Worst case in flight:** `t` at 7 digits (after about 16.7 min powered
  on, which a bench, full-to-empty flight and 60 s rest can reach), 4-digit
  V/Vm/Vc/I/Is/M, 3-digit D and S. That adds up to about 127 B. Still under 130,
  but only by about 3 B.
     - `Is` cannot go past 4 digits: PGA /4 saturates the shunt register at
  +/-16000, which is 8000 mA.
     - Fix: none needed. If the app disconnects late in the log, shorten a tag
  (for example ` Arm:` to ` A:`) or print `t` in deciseconds.

  2. [NOTE, analysis] PlutoPilot.cpp:20-24 (`inaBusMv`) - `Vm` is measured on
  the load side of the shunt, so the pack resistance from the arming and landing
  steps comes out about 20 mOhm too high.
     Rule: general (units and scale), and task 13 **Done when** ("pack R").
     - The INA219 bus register reads VIN- to GND. On the usual high-side wiring
  (I did not check the board), that is after the 20 mOhm R020 shunt.
     - So dVm/dIs = R_pack + 0.020 Ohm + wiring.
     - Fix: none in code. In the analysis, subtract 20 mOhm, or use Vpack = Vm +
  Is x 0.02 V/A. Worth a line in TESTING.md.

  3. [NOTE] PlutoPilot.cpp:26-30 (`inaShuntMa`) - The code prints whole mA, not
  the 0.5 mA steps TASKS.md:326 promises, and an I2C error reads as 0 mA.
     Rule: general (doc/code consistency).
     - `raw / 2` truncates toward zero, which loses the half-mA bit and biases
  readings low by up to 0.5 mA. At a 2 A hover that is about 0.02 % of the `Is`
  integral, so it does not matter against the charger.
     - An I2C error (0xFFFF, i.e. -1 / 2 = 0) cannot be told apart from 0 mA. At
  10 Hz, a dropped sample just leaves a silent gap in the integral.
     - Fix: either change TASKS.md to "1 mA steps", or print the raw count in
  0.5 mA units if the resolution matters.

  **Verified correct, no finding:**
  - **Linkage.** Only `drivers/ina219.c` exists. The Makefile entry
  `drivers/ina219.cpp` maps to `Build/.../drivers/ina219.o`, and the `%.o: %.c`
  rule with VPATH builds it from the `.c` file. The object's producer string is
  `GNU C17 14.2.1`. Neither `ina219.o` nor `PlutoPilot.o` contains the mangled
  `_Z14INA219_RegReadh`, so the symbol has C linkage and the `extern "C"`
  declaration matches. `ina219.c` is in `PRIMUSX2_DRIVERS`, which all three
  targets link, so #9 does not apply.
  - **Register scaling against `INA219_Init`** (BRNG 16 V, GAIN_4, 12-bit both
  ADCs, continuous shunt+bus):
    - Bus: bits 15..3 at 4 mV LSB, which does not depend on BRNG, so
  `(reg>>3)*4` is right.
    - Shunt: 10 uV LSB, sign-extended, which does not depend on PGA.
  `static_cast<int16_t>` gives the correct sign.
    - Sign matches the BMS: positive means discharge (battery.cpp:302 zeroes
  values <= 0).
    - Bit 0 is the math-overflow flag. The calibration register is never
  written, so it should not set. The check is harmless and matches
  `bus_voltage()`.
  - **I2C safety.** `plutoLoop` (mw.cpp:1162) and `updateINA219Voltage/Current`
  (mw.cpp:426/438) both run in `loop()`, the same main-loop context, so they
  cannot run at the same time. The driver is polled, and every wait is bounded
  by `I2C_LONG_TIMEOUT`. Two 2-byte reads every 100 ms at 400 kHz take about 0.3
  ms in total. The only extra exposure is an error-path
  `i2cRecoverPeripheral()` in the main loop, which the BMS reads already carry.
  Continuous 532 us conversions mean each read returns the latest single sample,
  which matches "no averaging".
  - **Conversions.** Every narrowing is an explicit `static_cast`. All values
  passed to `Monitor_Print(const char*, int)` are int or uint16_t.

  Checked and clean: 1, 6 (under budget, see note 1), 8 (bounded polled I2C, the
  existing pattern), 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15, 16, 17, 18. Items 2-5 and 7 do not
  apply to this hunk.

  - Item 14: there is no `build.log` at the repo root, so I did not run

  - The older battery-capacity-estimate diagnostic code in the same diff was out
  of scope.
  - The board schematic, to confirm VIN- is on the load side (note 2).

● It could be measured at boot, but I'd advise against it, for two reasons.

  1. It needs a load step, and only the motors can provide one. At rest the
  sensor is very quiet: ±2 mV and ±8 mA in log-1. Resistance is the voltage drop
  divided by a current change, though, and the board idles at a steady ~125 mA.
  Measuring it at boot means briefly pulsing the motors with props on, while
  you may be holding the drone. That is a safety question, and I wouldn't make
  the motors twitch unasked at power-up.

  2. A short step measures the wrong resistance. From log-1, on the same pack:

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────┐
  │                  How it is measured                  │ Resistance  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Fast voltage wobble against current wobble in hover  │ ~100-110 mΩ │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Step 1 s after arming                                │ 123-128 mΩ  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Steady hover and the settled 60 s rest after landing │ ~146 mΩ     │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────┘

  The warning voltage needs the steady value, and the extra part builds up over
  20-30 s of load. A pulse at boot would read about 25-30% low, and correcting
  it would need a factor learned from more packs.

  What I'd do instead is measure it in the first 30 s of each flight, from the
  rest voltage before arming to the hover voltage at ~30 s. That needs no extra
  action, measures the right quantity (150 mΩ here, ~105 on log-6, each matching
  its pack), and the warning isn't needed until minutes later. Until those 30 s
  have passed, the voltage check would assume a low resistance, which errs
  toward warning early rather than late.

  One cheap check for a later validation flight: arm and hold at idle for about
  3 s before takeoff. That shows what an idle-only step gives on each pack, with
  no code change.

  I added this to INVESTIGATION.md §5.6 as input for the task 5 decision. Task
  13 is still waiting on the charger's mAh.

> I will fly 2-3 more battery so that we can take an average for a fixed or we
  can do a dynamic resistance throughout the flight or few sec then use that,
  because I need to send mah remaining to the app so that the person flying
  knows how much battery capacity they have

● You don't need to choose fixed or dynamic before flying. The same log fields
  support both, so I'll replay each method over every log afterwards and let the
  numbers decide. Adding this as task 14.

  Changes from last flight:
  - Note the pack and the room temperature; resistance rises in the cold.
  - Leave it 20 s on the ground before arming.
  - If the motors idle after arming, hold them there for about 3 s before
    takeoff.
  - Keep 60 s untouched after landing, as before.
  - Note the charger's mAh after each flight.

  How resistance ties into the remaining mAh in the app: the charge count gives
  mAh used to within about 1-2%. Remaining is capacity minus used, and the real
  capacity is the weak part: last flight's pack holds about 567 mAh, not 600.
  The resistance lets the firmware turn the loaded voltage into a resting
  voltage and read that on the LiPo curve. That reading can correct the
  remaining figure during the flight, especially near the end, where the curve
  is steep.

  After the flights, I'll replay three methods on every log:
  - one fixed average resistance;
  - a resistance measured in each flight's first ~30 s;
  - a resistance re-estimated throughout the flight.

  For each I'll compare the remaining-mAh error over the flight and where the
  15% and 5% warnings would fire.

  My current lean is per-flight or continuous, because resistance already varies
  too much for one number. We have 100 against 146 mΩ between two packs, and
  100 against about 135 on the same pack on different days. An average of about
  120-125 would still warn at about 50% left on one pack and late on another.
  The survey will confirm or overturn that.

  Task 5 now waits on this survey. Task 13 still needs the charger's mAh for the
  pack you flew last. Once the logs are in, say next.

● Task 13 is closed, but the 502 mAh shows the current sensor reads about 5%
  above the charger.

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────┬────────────────┐
  │                                      │   This pack    │   log-6 pack   │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Raw sensor charge against the        │ 528 / 502 =    │ 563 / 534 =    │
  │ charger                              │ 1.053          │ 1.055          │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ Firmware's consumed mAh against the  │ 0.986          │ 0.99           │
  │ charger                              │                │                │
  └──────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────┴────────────────┘

  - It's systematic. Two packs agree to within 0.2%, and a LiPo gives back
    almost all the charge put in, so either the sensor or the charger is off by
    about 5%.
  - The auto-gain has been hiding it. Its -5% cancels the high reading, which is
    why the firmware's figure lands within 1-1.5% of the charger. Task 3
    removes the auto-gain, so without a fix the count would read about 5.5%
    high. That is just outside the ±5% target.
  - A likely cause on the sensor side is about 1 mΩ of PCB copper inside the 20
    mΩ shunt's measurement path, which would make it read 5% high. The charger's
    own accuracy is unknown, so I can't say yet which one is wrong.
  - This pack's real capacity is about 525 mAh on the charger's scale, against
    the 600 rating.

  I added task 15 to settle it: compare the sensor's current with a reference
  meter at two or more steady currents, ideally one near hover (about 4 A). If
  the sensor is about 5% high, task 3 adds a fixed per-board scale factor in
  place of the auto-gain. If the sensor agrees with the meter, the charger reads
  low and nothing changes. Task 3 now waits on task 15.

  For task 15, do you have a multimeter with a 10 A range, or a bench supply
  whose current display you trust? I'll agree the method with you when we start
  it. It can run any time, alongside the pack survey (task 14). Paste each
  survey flight into logs/log-2.txt onward, with the charger mAh, and say next.

● log-2 is in TESTING.md. The resistance result is clear, but the 420 mAh
  charger figure doesn't fit the other two packs.

  Resistance, older 600: about 157 mΩ in steady hover (148-163), and 167 mΩ from
  the first 30 s. So far the three packs read about 100 (log-6), 146 and 157
  mΩ.

  Warning replay on the last two flights (charge really left, seconds before
  landing):

  ┌───────────────────────┬────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┐
  │    Resistance used    │  Newer 600 (log-1):    │  Older 600 (log-2):    │
  │                       │   warning / critical   │   warning / critical   │
  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ Fixed 100 mΩ          │ 66% / 50%              │ 76% / 54%              │
  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ Fixed 135 mΩ (average │ 39% / 6%               │ 43% / 18%              │
  │  so far)              │                        │                        │
  ├───────────────────────┼────────────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
  │ Measured each flight, │ 10% (29 s) / 4.5%      │ 17% (37 s) / not       │
  │  first 30 s           │                        │ reached                │
  └───────────────────────┴────────────────────────┴────────────────────────┘

  On log-2 you landed with about 9% left, before the 5% level. A fixed average
  warns with about 40% left on both packs; per-flight resistance lands both
  warnings at 10-17%. Today's firmware warned at 22% on this flight, against
  15.5% on log-1 and about 3% on log-6.

  The 420 mAh doesn't fit. The sensor counted 480 mAh, which is 1.142 times the
  charger, against 1.053 and 1.055 on the other two packs.
  - The sensor can't change its scale between flights on the same board.
  - The start and end resting voltages put this pack at about 543 mAh from full
    to empty, close to the 567 of the last pack.
  - So the charge most likely stopped before full. An older, higher-resistance
    pack reaches the charger's cut-off sooner.
  - The earlier 430 mAh reading was the same kind of low outlier.

  The charger's mAh looks unreliable as a reference on its own, so task 15, the
  meter check, matters more now.

  Other notes:
  - The app takes off the moment you arm, so the 3 s idle hold can't be flown.
    I've dropped it; resistance comes from the first 30 s instead.
  - I didn't get this pack's label or the room temperature. Please note both for
    log-3.

  Next: log-3, another newer 600, and optionally log-4 on an 800 with capacity
  set to 800. Did the charger show a "full" or complete state for this pack? If
  it stopped on a timer or you unplugged it early, that explains the 420.

> on my charger it shows internal resistance of 100mOhms to 124mOhms

● The charger's figure fits our numbers once you account for what each one
  measures.

  The charger measures the pack's internal resistance with a very short current
  pulse at its own leads. That gives only the instant, ohmic part of the pack:
  100-124 mΩ.

  The drone's figure is measured at the INA219. On top of the pack it includes:
  - the 20 mΩ shunt;
  - the connector and board wiring;
  - polarization, which builds over the first 20-30 s under load.

  Lining the two up:

  ┌──────────┬────────────────┬───────────┬───────────┬────────────────────┐
  │          │ Fast step at   │ Less the  │ Charger   │ Steady / first 30  │
  │   Pack   │ landing ( +0.2 │  20 mΩ    │    IR     │   s ( what the     │
  │          │       s )      │   shunt   │           │  warning needs )   │
  ├──────────┼────────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ log-1,   │                │           │ within    │                    │
  │ newer    │ 126 mΩ         │ ~106      │ 100-124   │ 146 / 150          │
  │ 600      │                │           │           │                    │
  ├──────────┼────────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ log-2,   │                │           │ within    │                    │
  │ older    │ 151 mΩ         │ ~131      │ 100-124   │ 157 / 167          │
  │ 600      │                │           │           │                    │
  └──────────┴────────────────┴───────────┴───────────┴────────────────────┘

  So the fast step matches the charger's range, and steady hover adds another
  ~25-40 mΩ. The charger's IR can't be used directly for the warning voltage; it
  would compensate too little, like the fixed 100 mΩ that warned at 66-76%. But
  if the gap proves consistent across packs, it's a good cross-check that the
  drone's per-flight value is sane.

  The log-6 value (~100 mΩ including the shunt) doesn't fit this picture: it
  would mean a pack at ~60-80 mΩ. That log only had 0.1 V voltage steps, so I'll
  treat it as the weakest of the three.

  The register set-up (`INA219_Init`: PGA /4, 12-bit, 16 V range, continuous) is
  unchanged. No `double`, and no em or typographic dashes. The files keep their
  CRLF line endings, so the diff has no line-ending churn.

  **Makefile entry**
  - The object list is built at Makefile:475-476 with `$(basename
  $($(TARGET)_SRC))`, which strips the file extension. So `drivers/ina219.cpp`
  and `drivers/ina219.c` both give `Build/<T>/bin/drivers/ina219.o` and `.d`.
  - The `%.o: %.cpp` rule (Makefile:518) finds no `ina219.cpp` on VPATH, so make
  fell through to `%.o: %.c` (Makefile:524), which compiles with `$(C)
  $(CFLAGS)`. The file was therefore always compiled as C.
  - No other Makefile rule uses the extension from the source lists. The change
  is correct and does not alter the build.
  - Other entries in `PRIMUSX2_DRIVERS` and `DRONA_DRIVERS` already list `.c`
  files next to `.cpp` ones. `DRONA_DRIVERS` also lists
  `drivers/display_ug2864hsweg01` with no extension at all, which builds the
  same way; I left it alone.

  **Gate and memory (PRIMUS_X2_v1)**
  - Command: `bash .claude/skills/pluto-build/driver.sh --gate PRIMUS_X2_v1`,
  exit 0. Output: "no new warnings in src/", "ALL BUILDS PASSED".
  - Size: text 101040, data 1288, bss 13936.
  - Flash 99.9 KB / 256 KB (39%); RAM 14.8 KB / 40 KB (37%).
  - RAM change is 0, since there are no new statics.
  - The INA219 driver's code now totals 180 B (`ina219ReadReg` 32,
  `INA219_RegRead` 28, `INA219_ReadBus_mV` 36, `INA219_ReadShunt_10uV` 26,
  `bus_voltage` 34, `shunt_voltage` 28, `INA219_RegWrite` 26, `INA219_Init` 10).
  I did not rebuild the old tree, so there is no measured flash delta; my
  estimate is under 100 B added.
  - I2C cost per call is unchanged: one 2-byte read.

  **Points to check**
  1. The CNVR bit (bus register bit 1) is ignored, as before. In continuous mode
  the register always holds the last conversion, so a read never fails on it.
  Say if task 3 wants a "new sample" check.
  2. `INA219_RegRead` is still not declared in ina219.h (PlutoPilot.cpp declares
  it itself). I left it that way to keep the header change small.
  3. Before this change, a failed shunt read returned 0, which went into the
  average as 0 mA. The shim keeps that on purpose. Task 3 must switch to
  `INA219_ReadShunt_10uV` to actually skip failed reads, and then delete both
  shims.
  4. After my Edit-tool changes, an MSYS `sed -i` call stripped the CRs from
  ina219.c/.h. I put CRLF back with Python and checked the result: every line
  ends CRLF and `git diff --stat` shows only the real edits.

  2. [NOTE] src/main/drivers/ina219.c:47 - `INA219_RegRead` (and
  `INA219_RegWrite`/`INA219_Config`, which were already like this) has no
  prototype in `ina219.h`. The only other declaration is the hand-written one in
  `PlutoPilot.cpp:13` inside `extern "C"`, and it matches the definition
  (`uint16_t (uint8_t)`), so linkage is correct today. A future signature change
  would not be caught at compile time. Adding the prototype to the header is
  optional. This is not a defect, because the diagnostic is temporary.

  3. [NOTE] src/main/drivers/ina219.c:37 - the `#define INA219_BUS_OVF_BIT` sits
  between function bodies. Style only.

  Verified in detail:
  - **`bus_voltage` shim:**
    - I2C error: the old code got 0xFFFF from `INA219_RegRead`, saw bit 0 set
  and returned 0xFFFF. The new code returns 0xFFFF. Same.
    - OVF set: 0xFFFF in both. Same.
    - Otherwise both give `((reg>>3)*4)/100`, max 32764/100 = 327. The
  intermediate fits in uint16_t, so the new `(uint16_t)((uint32_t)(reg>>3)*4U)`
  loses nothing. Same.
  - **`shunt_voltage` shim:**
    - I2C error: the old code read 0xFFFF as -1, and -1*10/1000 = 0. The new
  code returns 0. Same.
    - A valid 0xFFFF reading gives 0 in both.
    - Negative readings: both compute `int16 * 10 / 1000` in `int`, which
  truncates toward zero (for example 0xFF9C (-100) gives -1 in both). The result
  range is +/-327 and fits in int16_t. Same.
  - **Conversions:** `(uint16_t)buffer[0] << 8 | buffer[1]` is cast explicitly.
  `(int16_t)reg` is an explicit implementation-defined narrowing, which is two's
  complement on GCC ARM. `busMv / 100U` and `reg & 0x0001U` work on promoted
  non-negative values, so GCC does not warn under -Wsign-conversion. The build
  log (21:29:14, newer than the sources at 21:28:22) shows only `lib/` CMSIS
  warnings for ina219.c, and `python tools/warnings.py check
  Build/PRIMUS_X2_v1/build.log` prints "no new warnings in src/".
  - **Output left untouched on false:** true for both new functions, and for
  `ina219ReadReg` (it returns before writing `*val`).
  - **C linkage:** the header's `extern "C" {` / `}` guards wrap all
  declarations, including the new ones. The callers are C++ (`main.cpp`,
  `sensors/battery.cpp`) and include the header, so linkage is correct.
  `ina219ReadReg` is `static`, so nothing is exported by mistake.
  - **Makefile:** `PRIMUSX2_DRIVERS` is shared by `PRIMUSX2_SRC`,
  `PRIMUS_X2_v1_SRC` and `PRIMUS_V5_SRC` (Makefile:349/357/365), so all three
  targets compile this file. `TARGET_OBJS` uses `$(basename ...)`, so the object
  is still `drivers/ina219.o`, built by `%.o: %.c` (Makefile:525) exactly as
  before. There is no `ina219.cpp` on disk. The build is unchanged for all three
  targets. The three targets define `INA219_Voltage`, and the code has no
  target-specific paths.
  - **Register set-up:** `INA219_Init` is not changed (16 V, PGA /4, 12-bit,
  continuous).
  - **I2C cost:** each shim still does exactly one 2-byte read, so the load on
  the shared I2C1 bus is unchanged.
  - **Banners:** `ina219.c` and `ina219.h` are preserved, with Last Modified and
  HISTORY lines added. The Makefile banner is updated. 2026-09-28 is a Monday,
  so the dates are correct.
  - **"Done when":** met for this task. PRIMUS_X2_v1 builds clean with no new
  `src/` warnings, the header comments give the units (mV / 4 mV LSB, 10 uV
  signed LSB, the false conditions), and the thin shim is allowed because task 3
  is separate.

  Checked and clean:

  Survey result, written up in INVESTIGATION.md §5.7 for task 5:
  - Resistance measured each flight over its first ~30 s, with a fixed

    at 10-17%.

    left on the two packs), and critical at 3.60-3.62 V.
  - The voltage pulls the remaining mAh down when it reads lower than the count.

  shunt reading in its native 10 µV steps. Each read reports whether it worked,

  - Measure it: do you have a multimeter with a 10 A range, or a bench supply

> ok resistance measure each flight will it effect the estimate battery which
  is measured at the start ? or will it be changed

● No. Measuring the resistance each flight doesn't change the estimate taken at
  plug-in. The resistance only matters later in the flight.

  At plug-in the board draws only about 125 mA, so the resistance correction is
  about 12-18 mV. On the LiPo curve that moves the estimate by under 1%, so a
  default value is fine there. The starting estimate comes from the resting
  voltage on the curve times the capacity you set, and it stays as it is.

  How the app's remaining mAh would behave, per the design in §5.7:

  ┌────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │     Phase      │             Remaining mAh sent to the app              │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Plugged in,    │ Start estimate: resting voltage on the curve ×         │
  │ disarmed       │ capacity                                               │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ First ~30 s of │ Start estimate minus the mAh counted; resistance being │
  │  flight        │  measured                                              │
  ├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Rest of the    │ The lower of the count and the voltage figure          │
  │ flight         │ (compensated voltage on the curve × capacity)          │
  └────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  - Mid-flight the count leads, because the curve is flat there and the voltage
    can't place the charge well.
  - Near the end, on a pack that holds less than its rating, the voltage figure
    drops below the count and takes over. The remaining then falls to about 0 as
    the pack empties, instead of showing 58 mAh on an empty pack as the last
    test did.
  - In flight the figure can only go down, never jump up.

  One improvement worth considering for task 4: the resting voltage just before
  arming is a better reading than the one at plug-in. A pack straight off the
  charger reads a little high until it settles, and one plugged back in right
  after a flight reads low. The firmware could refresh the start estimate from
  the last few seconds before the first arming, and it has to take that resting
  reading for the resistance measurement anyway.

  Want me to add that to task 4? Task 15 still needs your answer: a meter check,
  or drop it.

> so what's next task?

● The next task is 15: check the current sensor against a reference meter. It's
  the only one that can run: task 3 (the charge counter) waits on it, and
  everything after task 3 waits on that.

  - In RAM, the int16 shunt buffer (100 B) replaces the uint16 one (100 B). The
  shunt ring struct (16 B) and the gain float (4 B) are gone; `vBat_mV` (2 B),
  the int32 sum (4 B) and the head/count bytes (2 B) are new.

  ### Unit chain (exact expressions)

  **Voltage**
  - The bus reading is `busMv` in mV (4 mV LSB).
  - `vBat_mV = ring_avg_u16_get ( &vbatRawAvgRing, busMv )` gives a 50-sample
  mean in mV, floored to 1 mV.
  - `vBatRaw = ( uint16_t ) ( vBat_mV / 100U )` gives 0.1 V steps.

  **Shunt to current**
  - The shunt reading `shunt10uV` is an int16 with a 10 uV LSB (range +/-16000).
  - `vShunt10uVSum` is an int32; its magnitude is at most 50 x 32768, which is
  exact in float.
  - `avg10uV = ( float ) vShunt10uVSum / ( float ) vShunt10uVCount`, then `if (
  avg10uV < 0 ) avg10uV = 0` (the only clamp).
  - `mA = avg10uV * ( 0.01f / INA219_SHUNT_RESISTOR )`. The scale is 10 uV =
  0.01 mV and mV / ohm = mA, so it is 0.5 mA per LSB at 0.02 ohm.
  - `mA` is clamped to 65535 and `mAmpRaw = ( uint16_t ) ( mA + 0.5f )`.
  - `vShuntRaw = ( uint16_t ) ( avg10uV * 0.01f )` in mV, floored, at most 327.

  **Time to charge**
  - `dtUs = nowUs - last_us`, uint32 and wrap-safe.
  - `mA_us_accum += ( uint64_t ) mAmpWithGain * ( uint64_t ) dtUs`, in mA x us.
  - `mAhDrawn = min ( mA_us_accum / 3600000000ULL, 0xFFFF )`, because 1 mAh = 1
  mA x 3600 s = 3.6e9 mA x us.
  - `mAhRemain = EstBatteryCapacity > mAhDrawn ? EstBatteryCapacity - mAhDrawn :
  0`.
  - Nothing wraps: the uint64 accumulator at 65535 mA would take about 2.8e8 s
  to overflow.

  ### Things to know or check

  1. **`vBatRaw` can read one 0.1 V step higher than before.** It used to be the
  mean of per-sample floored 0.1 V codes, and error codes of 0xFFFF could enter
  that average. Now it is the floor of the mV mean, so it is equal or one step
  higher. The effect reaches the outside users, `handleBatteryConnected` (cell
  count and estimate) and the vBatComp line. It is a more correct figure; I did
  not change any threshold for it.
  2. **`vShuntRaw` changes at line 626.** It is now the floored mV of a signed
  average. Before, a negative shunt sample went into the uint16 ring as about
  65535 (a large spike), and positive samples were floored to whole mV. So the
  vBatComp line (battery.cpp:626, left as it was for task 5) now gets a sane
  value, but it is not bit-identical to the old behaviour.
  3. **`mAmpRaw` resolution and the zero case.** Resolution is now about 0.5 mA
  (rounded) instead of 50 mA steps. When the average is at or below 0, `mAmpRaw`
  is now set to 0. Before, it kept its stale value and only the gain path
  returned 0.
  4. **Reads and integration now run on every call.** A shunt read and an
  integration step happen on every `updateINA219Current` call (the 21 ms
  `IBATINTERVAL`). Before, a call with dt under 1 ms skipped the read and lost
  the time. The I2C cost per call is the same (one 2-byte read).
  5. **`armed` is unused.** `updateINA219Current` ignores it, but I kept it
  because `battery.h` and `mw.cpp` use the signature.
  6. **`mAmpWithGain` is now the same value as `mAmpRaw`.** It stays in the
  build because `API-Src/BMS.cpp` (`Bms_Get` Current) returns it, which is task
  6's to review.
  7. **Not touched:** `mw.cpp`, `handleBatteryConnected`, the SoC / fusion /
  warning code, `computeVbatComp_mV` and the vBatComp line.
  8. **Only PRIMUS_X2_v1 was built.** Nothing I changed is target-specific, but
  PRIMUS_V5 and PRIMUSX2 have not been compiled yet (that happens at commit).
  9. **Not done yet:** the reviewer confirmation and the bench check that `I` is
  within one 10 mA step, which are both in the "done when".

  ## Review: battery-soc-fix task 3 (5 files, 6 findings: 0 blocking, 1
  should-fix, 5 notes)

  The units, the wrap limits and the running sum all check out for task 3.
  Nothing is blocking. The one should-fix is a doc-sync gap. The notes are
  either new behaviour worth a decision or older defects that tasks 4, 5 and 6
  should pick up.

  **Boundaries confirmed against the code:**
  - **10 uV to mA:** `0.01f / 0.02f` is exactly 0.5 (the two floats have the
  same mantissa).
  - **us to mAh:** the divisor 3.6e9 is right (1 mA x 3600 s x 1e6 us/s).
  - **mV to 0.1 V:** `/100U`, floored.
  - **Shunt sum:** int32, at most 50 x 32768 = 1.64e6, well under 2^24, so the
  float conversion is exact.
  - **Charge accumulator:** at most 65535 mA x 21000 us = 1.4e9 per call, so the
  uint64 cannot overflow in practice.
  - **Elapsed time:** `nowUs - last_us` is wrap-safe, and it comes from the same
  `currentTime` that `mw.cpp:434` uses to schedule the call.
  - **Saturation and rounding:** `mAhDrawn` and `mAhRemain` saturate as
  specified. `mA + 0.5f` at 65535 truncates to 65535, which is in range.
  - **Shunt ring:** head and count are correct, the first 50 samples are
  averaged over the actual count, and `batteryInit` resets all four fields.
  - **First call:** it reads the shunt, then only sets the timestamps. On the
  first loop the voltage update (and `handleBatteryConnected`) runs before the
  current update, so `EstBatteryCapacity` is already set.
  - **Removed symbols:** no code anywhere reads them any more (`bus_voltage`,
  `shunt_voltage`, `_ina219_current_gain`, `ina219_auto_calibrate_current`,
  `vShuntRawAvgRing`, `mA_ms_accum`, `CURR_CAL_*`). The only mentions left are
  in docs and the CHANGELOG.
  - **`PlutoPilot.cpp`:** only the `mAmpRaw` extern comment and the `I:` print
  remain.

  1. [SHOULD-FIX] `docs/fw-development-reference/active-development/battery-soc-
  fix/PIPELINE_UPDATE.md:10-16` - "Items collected so far" has only the task 2
  bullets. Task 3 has no entry.
     Rule: pluto-rules "Before you call it done" §3 (active-development notes
  and `PIPELINE_UPDATE.md`)
     Failure: task 10 rewrites `Power_BMS_Pipeline.md` from this list, so it
  would miss these changes: the `vBat_mV` global, `vBatRaw` now floored from the
  mV average, the signed 10 uV average clamped once, the auto-gain removed, the
  `mA x us` counter, the saturating `mAhDrawn`/`mAhRemain`, and the new meaning
  of `vShuntRaw`.
     Fix: add a "Task 3" bullet group that mirrors the task 3 table in
  `CHANGES.md`.

  2. [GENERAL, note] `src/main/sensors/battery.cpp:220-228` and `:324-338` - the
  charge counter is never reset when a battery is reconnected. `mA_us_accum`
  and `mAhDrawn` persist, and the `init` static stays latched. The 50-sample
  voltage ring is not reset either.
     Rule: general (missing reset on state change)
     Failure: on the bench a pack swap without a power cycle (USB power, or the
  task 8 supply sweep dipping below 1.1 V) gives `mAhRemain = max(0, E_new -
  drawn_total)`. With the new saturation that reads 0 %, so the mAh SoC is 0 on
  a fresh pack. Before this change the subtraction wrapped to about 100 %.
  Separately, because the ring is not reset, a replug reaches
  `handleBatteryConnected` on a partly refilled average (about 14 samples at 4
  V), so the cell count and E are computed from roughly 1.1 V. That part is not
  new.
     Fix: in the task 4 reconnect path, clear `mA_us_accum`, `mAhDrawn` and the
  voltage ring on connect. No task covers the counter reset today.

  3. [GENERAL, note] `battery.cpp:241-244` and `:289-291` (with
  `mw.cpp:425/435`) - a failed read keeps the last value with no limit on how
  long. The `*LastServiced` timestamps are set whether or not the read
  succeeded.
     Rule: general (failsafe path / stale data)
     Failure: if the INA219 or I2C fails and stays failed, the frozen `mAmpRaw`
  is integrated forever, including while disarmed, so `mAhDrawn` keeps climbing.
  `vBatRaw` also freezes, so the battery is never seen as disconnected.
  `iFresh`/`vFresh` in `computeVbatComp_mV` cannot notice, because the
  timestamps still advance. Before, a failed read counted as 0 mA and about 6553
  V. Holding the last current is on the safe side for warnings in flight, but
  the failure is now silent.
     Fix: count consecutive failures. After about 12 of them (about 250 ms),
  mark the reading stale, and either stop integrating while disarmed or expose a
  flag for task 5.

  4. [GENERAL, note, older defect] `src/main/mw.cpp:453` - CRSF gets `mAmpRaw /
  10`, which is in 10 mA units. `crsf.c:49,353` documents that field as 0.1 A,
  so CRSF current reads 10 times too high. `frsky.c:418`, `hott.c:248` and
  `smartport.c:334` use the same `/10`. Task 3 did not change the unit
  (`mAmpRaw` was mA before and after).
     Rule: pluto-rules #10 (scale at a boundary), older defect
     Fix: `/100` for CRSF. Belongs to task 6 ("CRSF must not break").

  5. [SHOULD-FIX, deferred to task 6] `src/main/API-Src/BMS.cpp:33` -
  `Bms_Get(Current)` now returns the value without the gain, about 5 % above the
  old one (the gain always settled at 0.95).
     Rule: pluto-rules #15 (public API behaviour)
     Fix: log it in task 6 and make the wiki / `API_Version` decision there.
  `CHANGES.md` already mentions it.

  6. [NOTE] `battery.cpp:94,247` - `VBATT_PRESENT_THRESHOLD_MV` is in 0.1 V
  units, and the test is a strict `>`. The actual presence threshold is
  therefore `vBatRaw >= 11`, which is 1.1 V, not the "10 = 1.0 V" in the
  comment. Also, `battery.cpp:407,626` still use `vBatRaw * 100` (100 mV steps)
  even though `vBat_mV` now exists.
     Rule: pluto-rules #10 / style (unit in the name)
     Fix: task 4 should rename the define (or fix its comment) and settle on 1.0
  vs 1.1 V. Task 5 should switch the vBatComp paths to `vBat_mV`.

  **Checked and clean:**

  - **Per-call cost:** every 21 ms there is one 2-byte I2C read per sensor, one

● Agent "Review task 3 charge counter" finished · 3m 47s

● Task 3, the charge counter, is done and the reviewer found nothing blocking.
  It builds clean on PRIMUS_X2_v1: no new warnings, three old ones in
  battery.cpp gone, flash 99.8 KB and RAM 14.8 KB.

  What changed:
  - Current: averaged from the sensor's exact reading, with its sign, in 0.5 mA
    steps instead of 50 mA steps. Failed reads are skipped instead of counted
    as 0.
  - Auto-gain: removed, with no correction factor in its place.
  - mAh count: integrated in microseconds, so no time between samples is lost.
  - Remaining mAh: stops at 0 instead of wrapping to about 65,000. That removes
    the jump to 54-58% on an empty pack.
  - Voltage: now kept to the mV in a new value, vBat_mV. Telemetry, the OLED and
    the app still read vBatRaw in 0.1 V steps, unchanged.
  - Compatibility shims: the two old driver functions from task 2 are gone.

  The reviewer's notes, all attached to later tasks:
  - Task 4: reset the mAh count when a pack is swapped without power-cycling.
    Otherwise the fresh pack would show 0 remaining.
  - Task 5: a dead current sensor is currently silent. The firmware keeps
    counting the last current forever, even disarmed, and never sees the battery
    as disconnected. Task 5 should flag readings as stale after about 250 ms of
    failures.
  - Task 6: Bms_Get(Current) now returns a value about 5% higher, because the
    gain is gone. That needs a wiki note and a decision on the API_Version bump.
  - Task 6, not being fixed: CRSF current reads 10 times too high. That's an
    older defect, and CRSF units were ruled out of scope on 26 Sep.
  - Task 8: the bench check of the current against the supply's ammeter moves
    into the bench sweep.

  The task 3 changes are also listed in PIPELINE_UPDATE.md for the docs update
  at commit.

  Next is task 4, the start estimate at plug-in. It will use an averaged resting
  voltage on the LiPo curve times the capacity you set, plus the cell count, no
  wrap below 3.0 V, the reset on pack swap and the presence threshold.

  Decision for you before I start: should the start estimate also be refreshed
  from the resting voltage just before the first arming? That catches a pack
  plugged in straight off the charger (reads high) or right after a flight
  (reads low). Say next with your answer.

  One question left before I start task 4: should the start estimate be
  refreshed from the resting voltage just before the first arming?

  - Yes: the estimate taken at plug-in is replaced once, just before the first
    arming, by a reading from the last few seconds disarmed. The resistance
    measurement takes that resting reading anyway. It corrects a pack plugged in
    straight off the charger (reads a little high) or right after a flight
    (reads low).
  - No: the plug-in reading alone is used, averaged over the first second or so
    after boot. It's simpler and one step less to test.

  ## Task 4 report: plug-in estimate from a settled average, LiPo curve and cell
  count

  Task 4 is done. The PRIMUS_X2_v1 build passes the gate with no new warnings in
  `src/`, and it clears 5 old warnings in battery.cpp. The only file changed is
  `E:\08-DRONA-AVIATION\99-Dump\MagisV2\src\main\sensors\battery.cpp`: CRLF is
  kept (746/746), no non-ASCII characters were added (the two `mΩ` comments were
  already there), and there is a new HISTORY line "Plug-in estimate: settled
  average, LiPo curve, cell count". The Last Modified date was already Mon, 28th
  Sep 2026, By AJ. Nothing was committed.

  ### Changes (for CHANGES.md)
  | Where (battery.cpp) | Change | Why |
  |---|---|---|
  | :81-82 | `VBATTERY_STABLE_DELAY` removed | `delay ( 40 )` is gone; the
  connect path no longer blocks. |
  | :84-98 | New constants: `VBATT_CELL_DETECT_MV` 4400, `VBATT_MAX_CELLS` 3,
  `VBATT_PLUGIN_SAMPLES` 24, `BATTERY_REST_R_MOHM` 120 (comment: ~125 mA idle,
  100-160 mOhm from tasks 13/14). New file-static state: `batteryEstimateReady`,
  `plugInSamples` (uint8), `plugInSum_mV` (uint32). | Settling, rest-voltage
  correction, cell rule. |
  | :100-115 | `lipoCurvePoint_t` and `static constexpr lipoRestCurve [ 21 ]`
  (in flash, 84 B), with a `static_assert` that it has 21 points | The
  INVESTIGATION.md 5.5 curve. |
  | :125 | `VBATT_PRESENT_THRESHOLD_MV` (0.1 V units, strict `>`, which really
  meant 1.1 V) replaced by `VBATT_PRESENT_MV 1000U`, compared against `vBat_mV`
  | Connects at >= 1000 mV and disconnects below it (task 3 review item). |
  | :213-238 | New `lipoCellRestFraction ( uint16_t cell_mV )` returns a float
  0..1. It interpolates linearly: 1.0 at >= 4200 mV, 0 at <= 3270 mV, at most 20
  steps. It is marked `[[maybe_unused]]` and sits outside `#ifdef
  INA219_Current`. | Task 5 reuses it, and PRIMUSX2 has no INA219_Current, so
  without the attribute it would warn there. |
  | :240-261 | New `setCellCountAndThresholds ( pack_mV )`: cells = ceil ( mV /
  4400 ) clamped to 1..3, then critical and warning thresholds = cells x
  per-cell config x 100, with explicit casts. | The cell count is now set before
  the thresholds that use it, and 4.20-4.35 V always gives 1. |
  | :263-285 | `handleBatteryConnected ( )` rewritten: sets BATTERY_OK, capacity
  and the per-cell `batteryMaxVoltage` as before, then cells and thresholds
  from `vBat_mV`. It zeroes E and resets the ready flag and the settle
  accumulator. | Non-blocking; the estimate is made later. |
  | :287-316 | New `handleBatteryPlugInEstimate ( )`: avg = sum / 24, then it
  refreshes cells and thresholds from that settled average. Under `#ifdef
  INA219_Current`: rest = avg + mAmpRaw x 120 / 1000 (uint32), per cell = rest /
  cells, E = cap x fraction + 0.5, clamped to cap, cast to uint16. Then
  `batteryEstimateReady = true`. | Estimate from the curve, with no wrap. |
  | :318-327 | `handleBatteryDisconnected ( )` unchanged, plus it clears
  `batteryEstimateReady`. | Design item 7. |
  | :339-365 | `updateINA219Voltage ( )`: the read result is kept in `busOk`;
  presence is checked on `vBat_mV` against `VBATT_PRESENT_MV`. In a new third
  `else if`, while present, not ready and on a good read, it adds `busMv` to the
  sum and counts it. At 24 samples (~0.5 s at 21 ms) it calls the estimate,
  once. | Averages the first ~0.5 s, non-blocking. The connect sample itself is
  not counted. |
  | :672-674 | `updateBatteryStateSoc ( )`, BATTERY_OK case: the OK to WARNING
  transition now also needs `batteryEstimateReady`. | The warning-window gate
  (item 6 below). |

  **Two points that differ from the design as written:**
  - **Settle average.** The estimate uses its own sum of the 24 samples read
  after connect, not `vBat_mV`. At a fresh boot this is the same number (the
  ring then holds only those samples plus the connect sample). It avoids a
  skewed estimate if the ring ever held samples from before the connect, for
  example with the board on USB before the pack went in. The design used
  `vBat_mV`; revert to it if you prefer.
  - **Cell count computed twice.** It is set at connect from `vBat_mV`, as the
  design says, and set again from the settled average when the estimate is made.
  - **`batteryMaxVoltage` stays per cell** (4200), because
  `MSP_VOLTAGE_METER_CONFIG` (serial_msp.cpp:1224) sends it next to the per-cell
  min/warning settings. On 1S this makes no difference. Task 5 owns the
  thresholds.

  ### E for capacity 600 at ~125 mA idle
  This assumes the bus reads the rest voltage minus 15 mV (I x 120 mOhm), so the
  code's correction brings it back to the rest voltage. The values come from a
  float32 host replica of the code and were checked by hand against the curve.

  | Rest V | 4.30 | 4.20 | 4.10 | 4.00 | 3.80 | 3.50 | 3.20 | 3.00 | 2.90 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Curve % | 100 | 100 | 88.33 | 77.5 | 40 | 3.38 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
  | E (mAh) | 600 | 600 | 530 | 465 | 240 | 20 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

  ### Cells
  | Pack V | 4.20 | 4.35 | 6.0 | 8.4 | 9.0 | 12.6 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Cells | 1 | 1 | 2 | 2 | 3 | 3 |

  4.400 V gives 1 and 4.401 V gives 2. Below 4.4 V, including 1.0 V, it is
  always 1, so the count is never 0 while a pack is present.

  ### Warning window (item 6)
  - **What happens without a gate.** For ~0.5 s after connect the state is
  BATTERY_OK with E = 0, so `mAhRemain` = 0 and `soc_from_mAh` = 0. The fused
  SoC is then socV x (1 - wAh/100).
  - **Why that can matter.** `wAhPrev` starts at 55 and `BMS_Update` runs every
  loop. In the first ~13 calls the fused value is 0.45 x socV, so it can reach
  <= 18% at a `vBatComp` around 3.3-3.42 V. A ready estimate there (curve ~2-4%)
  would give ~20%.
  - **Result.** So E = 0 could, on the margin, trigger `set_FSI ( Low_battery
  )`, `BEEPER_BAT_LOW` and later `BatteryWarningMode`.
  - **The gate.** Only the BATTERY_OK to BATTERY_WARNING condition
  (battery.cpp:674) now needs `batteryEstimateReady`. That transition is the
  only path to `set_FSI`, the beeper and `BatteryWarningMode`.
  - **Still running during the window:** `ENABLE_ARMING_FLAG ( OK_TO_ARM )`, the
  fusion and `soc_Fused`.
  - **After ready.** The window leaves `socFusedPrev` at about 0.7 x socV. That
  value can be <= 18 only at `vBatComp` <= ~3.22 V, where the curve gives <= ~1%
  and the real estimate would also warn. So nothing false carries past the
  gate.
  - **Cosmetic side effect:** `soc_Fused` reads low for ~0.5 s after power-up.

  ### Gate and memory
  - `driver.sh --gate PRIMUS_X2_v1`: "no new warnings in src/", ALL BUILDS
  PASSED. The gate lists 5 old battery.cpp warnings as fixed; per the gate, the
  baseline gets refreshed once these are committed.
  - Size: text 101368 B, data 1280 B, bss 13936 B. Flash is 100.2 KB / 256 KB,
  against 99.8 KB (rounded) recorded after task 3, so roughly +0.4 KB. I did not
  rebuild the pre-change tree for an exact delta.
  - RAM: 14.8 KB / 40 KB, +6 B bss (`plugInSum_mV` 4, `plugInSamples` 1,
  `batteryEstimateReady` 1).
  - New symbols: `lipoRestCurve` 84 B (flash) and `setCellCountAndThresholds`
  100 B. `lipoCellRestFraction` and the estimate are inlined, which makes
  `updateINA219Voltage` 468 B.

  ### Unsure / for later
  - **Bench (task 8):** the +15 mV rest correction assumes 120 mOhm. At 100 or
  160 mOhm the error is only 2-5 mV.
  - **Presence level:** it moved from an effective 1.1 V to 1.0 V, a tiny
  change.
  - **Behaviour change for task 5:** the warning transition is held off for ~0.5
  s after connect.
  - **Not done here:** `graphify update` / `graph_labels.py`. Run them at the
  topic's graph-refresh task.

● The deviations from my brief are reasonable; the main one is a separate sum of
  the 24 post-connect samples instead of vBat_mV. Checking the new estimate
  path before the reviewer:

  ## Review: battery-soc-fix task 4, plug-in estimate (1 file:

  I found no blocking defects. Each of the four findings below was confirmed
  against the current code.

  1. [GENERAL, low]
  E:\08-DRONA-AVIATION\99-Dump\MagisV2\src\main\sensors\battery.cpp:741-743 (the
  cause is the new window at :281) - For the ~0.5 s before the estimate is
  ready, the reported SoC and mAh-remaining values are wrong.
     Rule: general (reported values during the new window)
     Failure: after connect, `EstBatteryCapacity` is 0, so `mAhRemain` is 0
  (:457-461) and `soc_from_mAh ( )` gives 0. `fuse_soc_vbatComp_smart` then
  moves wAh to 30 and fused becomes about 0.7 x socV. When disarmed a drop is
  not rate-limited (:646-651), so `soc_Fused` falls: to about 64 on a 4.1 V
  pack, about 48 on a 3.8 V pack. `soc_Fused` and `mAhRemain = 0` go out over
  MSP (serial_msp.cpp:1000-1001), CRSF (mw.cpp:453) and `Bms_Get ( mAh_Remain
  )`. After the estimate lands they climb back at +1 per `BMS_Update`, which
  runs every loop (the mw.cpp:442 comparison is always true), so this is a
  visible dip of about 0.6 s. The :674 gate does stop it becoming a false
  warning: with the default config, fused <= 18 needs vBatComp <= ~3.23 V, which
  is below the curve's 0%.
     Fix: in `BMS_Update`, skip the fusion and state update (hold `soc_Fused`)
  until `batteryEstimateReady`. This can go into task 5.

  2. [NOTE] battery.cpp:358-364, :674 - If the INA219 bus reads fail for good
  after a connect, the estimate never completes, `batteryEstimateReady` stays
  false, and the low-battery warning is off for the whole session.
     Rule: general (failsafe path)
     Failure: a failed read skips both the sample sum and the counter, so the
  24-sample condition is never met. In that state the voltage and current paths
  are blind too, so this only matters for a dead sensor.
     Fix: optionally cap the attempts (e.g. 48 calls), then estimate from the
  samples collected or mark the estimate ready.

  3. [NOTE] battery.cpp:260-262 - The comment says "<= 3 x 255 x 100, fits
  uint16", but 3 x 255 x 100 = 76500, which is larger than 65535.
     Rule: 10 (scale at a narrowing cast)
     Failure: this only happens when 3 cells are detected and an MSP-set
  per-cell value is over 218 (21.8 V). It cannot happen on 1S or 2S. The value
  wraps silently in the `static_cast< uint16_t >`.
     Fix: correct the comment, or constrain the product to 0xFFFF.

  4. [NOTE, existing code, out of task 4] battery.cpp:320 -
  `handleBatteryDisconnected ( )` sets `batteryCellCount = 0`, and frsky.c:376
  divides by it. This can only happen while no battery is present (for example
  on USB power). While a battery is present the count is always 1..3: 1 at boot,
  and `setCellCountAndThresholds` clamps it at connect and at the estimate.
     Fix (later): leave it at 1 on disconnect.

  Verified correct (the specific checks you asked for):
  - **Curve table:** matches INVESTIGATION.md §5.5, all 21 points. 4200 gives
  1.0, 4199 gives 0.999, 4150 gives exactly 0.95, 3271 gives about 0.0001, 3270
  and below give 0. At most 20 loop steps. The integer differences promote to
  positive `int` before the float cast.
  - **Estimate maths:** rest = avg + mAmpRaw (mA) x 120 mOhm / 1000 = mV, which
  is about +15 mV at 125 mA. Per cell = rest / cells, with cells >= 1 at that
  point. E = capacity x fraction + 0.5, clamped to capacity, so 100% gives
  exactly the capacity and 0% gives 0 with no uint16 wrap. `plugInSamples` is >=
  24 at the division, and `plugInSum_mV` is at most 24 x 32764.
  - **Cell rule:** ceil(mV / 4400) gives 1 at 4200, 4300, 4350 and 4400 mV and 2
  at 4401. 0 is clamped to 1 and the maximum is 3. The count is set before the
  thresholds on both calls (:278 and :297).
  - **updateINA219Voltage paths:**
    - A first sample of 0 from the ring only delays the connect.
    - The connect sample itself is not summed.
    - A failed read is not summed and cannot trigger a connect or disconnect.
    - A USB-powered plug-in ramps the 50-sample average but sums raw samples.
    - Presence is `vBat_mV >= 1000` mV, with the absent test `< 1000`. This
  matches the intent.
    - A disconnect clears `batteryEstimateReady`, and a reconnect resets the sum
  and the counter.
  - **Outside users, units unchanged:**
    - serial_msp.cpp:1224 reads `batteryMaxVoltage` per cell in mV.
    - failsafe.cpp only has an `extern`.
    - display.c and frsky.c use `vBatRaw` in 0.1 V.
    - API-Src/BMS.cpp returns `EstBatteryCapacity` in mAh.
    - The PlutoPilot.cpp E/Cap/Cells line prints after Developer Mode is
  switched on, well after 0.5 s.
  - **Other alarm and arming paths during the window:** no other code sets
  `Low_battery` or `LowBattery_inFlight`, or writes `BatteryWarningMode` or the
  BAT beepers. `set_FSI` for these is only in `updateBatteryStateSoc`.
  mw.cpp:562 only resets. WARNING and CRITICAL can only be reached through the
  gated OK -> WARNING step. The state is `BATTERY_OK` in the window, so
  `OK_TO_ARM` stays set: arming is not blocked, and ledstrip/hott see OK.
  - **PRIMUSX2 (no INA219_Current):** `lipoCellRestFraction` is
  `[[maybe_unused]]`, and the unused namespace-scope `constexpr` does not warn
  in C++. The estimate sets ready with E = 0, the same as before. This target
  was not built (see below).

● Task 4, the start estimate at plug-in, is done. The reviewer found nothing
  blocking, and the build is clean on PRIMUS_X2_v1: flash 100.2 KB, RAM 14.8 KB,
  five old warnings in battery.cpp gone.

  What changed:
  - No 40 ms pause at plug-in. The firmware collects about 0.5 s of voltage
    samples (24 readings) instead.
  - Start estimate from the LiPo curve: capacity × the curve's charge fraction
    at the resting voltage, in place of the straight line. It can't wrap below
    3.0 V.

  - Battery presence is detected at 1.0 V, with the threshold now correctly

    still 0.

● I'll pull the actual loaded voltages from the two flights at the moments the

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Yes. In hover (about 4.3 A) on these packs, the proposed thresholds fire at
  roughly 3.0-3.1 V for the warning and about 2.95 V for critical, measured
  under load:

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬─────────────┐
  │             │  Warning (3.745 V    │  Critical (3.60 V    │  Last 2 s   │
  │   Flight    │ corrected) fires at, │ corrected) fires at, │   before    │
  │             │      under load      │      under load      │   landing   │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ log-1,      │ 3.10 V, 60 s before  │ 2.96 V, 1 s before   │             │
  │ newer 600,  │ landing              │ landing              │ 2.95 V      │
  │ ~150 mΩ     │                      │                      │             │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼─────────────┤
  │ log-2,      │ 3.02 V, 44 s before  │ not reached; landed  │             │
  │ older 600,  │ landing              │ with ~9% left        │ 2.89 V      │
  │ ~167 mΩ     │                      │                      │             │
  └─────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴─────────────┘

  Why the firmware won't compare the loaded voltage directly with a fixed 3.1 or
  2.9 V. The loaded voltage depends on the current and on the pack as well as
  on the charge left:
  - Between packs: at the same 17% left, the higher-resistance pack (log-2) read
    about 80 mV lower (3.02 against 3.10 V). A fixed 3.1 V would have warned on
    log-2 at about 30% left instead of 17%.
  - With the current: when you ease off the throttle (descending, or lighter
    hover), the current drops and the voltage jumps back up. A loaded-voltage
    warning would clear, and could flicker on and off. In log-1 the voltage came
    back from 2.95 to 3.47 V within 0.2 s of landing.

  So the firmware adds back current × resistance to get the pack's resting
  voltage, then compares that with 3.745 V and 3.60 V. At hover current on these
  packs, those land where you expect, around 3.0-3.1 V and 2.9-2.95 V.

  About the "critical at ~2.9 V": on log-1 critical fired only 1 s before
  landing, because you flew it right down to empty. On log-2 you landed at 2.89
  V under load with about 9% still left, so critical never came. On these packs,
  about 2.9 V under load marks the practical end, with little margin left.

  Your settings: the stored configuration has warning 3.2 V and minimum 3.0 V
  per cell, which are loaded-voltage style values. Task 5 would stop using those
  two as the trigger, which also means the app's voltage settings would no
  longer move the warning. If you'd rather keep them as a user-adjustable option
  (for example, a loaded-voltage backstop at 2.9 V), tell me, and I'll include
  it when task 5 starts.

> dont refer the log as per the system what is the criteria

● Here is the rule as the firmware would apply it after task 5. It is a proposal
  until you confirm it when task 5 starts. The debounce and hysteresis values
  are my suggestions and have not been measured.

  What it uses:
  - V: battery voltage from the sensor, in mV.
  - I: battery current, in mA.
  - C: the capacity set in the app, in mAh.
  - E: the start estimate at plug-in, which is C × the LiPo curve's charge
    fraction at the resting voltage.
  - Used: the mAh counted since power-up.

  1. Pack resistance R, measured once per power-up
  - Rest point: the average voltage and current while disarmed, just before
    arming.
  - Load point: the average voltage and current between about 25 and 35 s after
    arming.
  - Formula: R = (V_rest − curve drop − V_load) / (I_load − I_rest). The curve
    drop is how far the LiPo curve's voltage falls for the mAh used in those 30
    s.
  - Limits: R is clamped to 80-250 mΩ and kept until power-off.
  - Before R is known, only the mAh count can trigger a warning.

  2. Compensated voltage
  - Vcomp = (V + I × R) per cell, averaged over about 1 s. It estimates what the
    pack would read at rest.

  3. Warnings

  ┌───────────┬──────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
  │   Level   │      Fires when either       │           Action            │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ Low       │ Vcomp ≤ 3.745 V, or          │ beeper, and the app's       │
  │ battery   │ remaining ≤ 15% of C         │ low-battery flag            │
  ├───────────┼──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ Critical  │ Vcomp ≤ 3.60 V, or remaining │ beeper, and the app's       │
  │           │  ≤ 5% of C                   │ critical flag               │
  └───────────┴──────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘

  - Debounce: the condition must hold for about 1-2 s, so a throttle punch
    doesn't trigger it.
  - Latch: once set in flight, a level stays set until landing. After landing it
    clears only if Vcomp comes back more than about 50 mV above its threshold.

  4. Remaining mAh sent to the app
  - Count: E − Used, never below 0.
  - Voltage: once R is known, curve(Vcomp) × C.
  - Sent to the app: the lower of the two, and it never goes up while armed.
  - SoC: remaining ÷ C.

  5. What the thresholds mean under load
  - At the moment of warning, the loaded voltage is 3.745 V − I × R.
  - At critical, it is 3.60 V − I × R.
  - At a hover current of about 4.3 A and R of 150-170 mΩ, that comes to about
    3.0-3.1 V loaded for low battery and 2.9-2.95 V for critical.
  - At a lower current the loaded voltage at the same charge is higher, which is
    why the rule compensates instead of using a fixed loaded voltage.

  6. Guards
  - No warning in the first ~0.5 s after power-up, until E is ready.
  - If the sensor stops answering for more than about 250 ms, its readings count
    as stale. The count then stops while disarmed, and the voltage check is
    skipped.

> so the low battery will trigger at 3.1v or less then 3.745v ?

● Both, depending on the current. 3.745 V is not a voltage you will see measured
  in flight. The trigger is:

  V + I × R ≤ 3.745 V

  - V is the voltage the sensor measures right now.
  - I is the current right now.
  - R is the pack's resistance, measured in the first 30 s of the flight.

  The measured voltage at which it fires depends on the current:

  ┌──────────────────────┬──────────┬───────┬──────────────────────────────┐
  │      Situation       │ Current  │   R   │  Fires when the measured V   │
  │                      │    I     │       │           drops to           │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Hover                │ 4.3 A    │ 150   │ 3.10 V                       │
  │                      │          │ mΩ    │                              │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Hover, older pack    │ 4.3 A    │ 170   │ 3.01 V                       │
  │                      │          │ mΩ    │                              │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ Gentle descent       │ 2.0 A    │ 150   │ 3.45 V                       │
  │                      │          │ mΩ    │                              │
  ├──────────────────────┼──────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────────┤
  │ On the ground,       │ 0.13 A   │ 150   │ 3.73 V                       │
  │ disarmed             │          │ mΩ    │                              │
  └──────────────────────┴──────────┴───────┴──────────────────────────────┘

  So in hover the low-battery warning comes at about 3.0-3.1 V measured. On the
  ground, with almost no current, the same charge level shows about 3.73-3.745
  V. Either way it marks the same thing: about 15% of the pack left.

  Critical works the same way with 3.60 V. In hover that's about 2.9-2.95 V
  measured, and about 3.6 V at rest.
